# Binding decisions

This document is the current binding owner and supervisor direction; it wins over every other guidance file.
Compacted 2026-09-18 under the owner's instruction to remove repetition and retired decisions.
Full unchanged chronology: `.claude/decisions-archive/2026-09-18-full-chronology.md`.
This document replaces `.claude/RULES.md`; historical evidence remains in the archive.
Append new decisions chronologically in Part D, using the next free ID: **D683**.
The supervisor re-compacts periodically (D552).
Citations identify archival decisions or named findings; `(owner)` marks owner-originated direction; `(verify)` preserves an unresolved interpretation.
Pre-D649 commit IDs (before the 2026-09-17 purge) need translation via `.claude/consensus/purge-2026-09-17-commit-map.txt`; post-purge IDs are current.

## Part A — Standing rules

### Licensing and provenance

- (owner) Commit/distribute only work with redistribution authorization; keep imported copyrighted rules and private content out of public git/dist. User-local possession/import/export is allowed. [D3/D57/D59/D330]
- (owner) Public licensed sources may include SRD 5.2.1, SRD 5.1, A5E SRD, CC-BY 4.0, CC0/public domain, MIT and Apache, with actual terms, per-source dist attribution and per-row provenance; NC and ORC/OGL-only material are not ordinary public fixtures. [D59/D367/D395]
- (owner) Segregate approved CC-BY-SA conversions in `content/cc-by-sa/` under their own license; never feed SA fixtures to KB authoring/prompts. OGL text belongs only in a complete `docs/ogl/`-style quarantine with OGL 1.0a and exact Section 15 chain; neither quarantined text nor terms leak into engine code/other content. [D176/D395.2/D396.supervisor]
- (owner) Include A5E's required attribution: “This work includes material taken from the A5E System Reference Document (A5ESRD) by EN Publishing and available at A5ESRD.com, based on Level Up: Advanced 5th Edition, available at www.levelup5e.com.” [D367]
- (owner) Code is MIT; generated art and all generator outputs are CC0 1.0; original public docs are CC-BY-4.0; SRD retains its CC-BY attribution. Include license texts/provenance in dist and verify clean distribution; never imply cross-relicensing. [D126/D290/D547]
- (owner) Maintain NOTICE at every licensed-content merge, including original homebrew and 3.5-SRD concept ancestry; attribution must be reachable from every SRD-rendering screen and included in print, exports and agent-readable blocks. [D125/D191/D209/F1]
- Pin the official SRD 5.2.1 PDF SHA-256; keep verbatim extracts in `docs/srd/source/` and extraction commands/pages/hashes in SOURCE.md. Verify actual source shapes, including two-column/wrapped text, before enforcing counts or claiming provenance. [D17/D78/D303/F6/F26/F27]
- (owner) BG3/Larian/wiki evidence, non-SRD builds, oracle overlays and real private spell exemplars stay private; public mechanics/fixtures use neutral names and independently designed generalizations, not disguised copies or map/beat clones. [D260.9/D318/D353/D370/D373.9/D373.16/D417/D491/D492/D500]
- (owner) Keep audit evidence private; public Board reports provide licensing outcomes and lawful methodology without identifying external d4 fidelity builds. Issue reports must warn against nonredistributable text and redact it while retaining the report. [D244/D250/D290]
- (owner) Product identity excludes D&D/Dungeons & Dragons/protected game marks except required notices and factual citations; do not redact owner quotations merely because a cited non-SRD name explains non-bundleability. [D114/D178/D21/D529]
- (owner) Art research stays in `~/dnd-research-private/art-research`, with no remote/push; retain links/descriptions, not third-party images, in source-aware `judges-only/<perspective>/<model>.md`; implementers get only leakage-cleared `clean-room/` principles. [D529]
- (owner) Clean-room writing uses original language, no franchise names/assets/palettes/lifted phrasing; numeric thresholds need two independent sources or an explicitly labeled PROJECT DEFAULT with validation. SRD size mechanics are permitted licensed domain content. [D529/F: clean-room synthesis constraints, 2026-09-05]
- (owner) Open clean-room documents only after Codex and independent Opus-medium both say CLEAN; `clean-room/final/` v3 is current art authority, with `judges-only/` still closed. [D542/F: clean-room open, 2026-09-05]
- (owner) Image models supply private reference sheets only, never shipped pixels/public fixtures; record prompt/model/date/asset in `references/LOG.md`, reject structural copying at native size, and separate reference viewers from implementers; any specific viewing exception must be recorded. [D558/D559]
- (owner) Convert reference observations into repository-generated SVG/procedural art; successful-reference logging is atomic only after PNG signature/dimensions pass, failed fetch leaves output/log unchanged, and a tool stops only a server it started. [D559/F: successful reference logging, 2026-09-05]
- (owner) ComfyUI is CPU-only at `~/comfyui`, bound to localhost away from 4173; default model is FLUX.2 Klein 4B Q8_0 via `UnetLoaderGGUF` and `flux-2-klein-4b-Q8_0.gguf`. Keep model license records after deleting weights. [D560/F: local reference default, 2026-09-06]
- (owner) Krea work remains parked; resuming requires license verification/hash in `models/LICENSES.md`; noncommercial generation is LOCAL REFERENCE ONLY with `--local-reference-only`, and licenses forbidding local generation refuse. SD1.5 is fallback only if both candidates fail. [D560.1/D560.2]
- (owner) Fireworks is language-only after local-image selection; authorized Fireconnect credential route is `fireconnect key export --home /home/vagrant`. Routes: FABLE/OPUS → `accounts/fireworks/routers/kimi-k3-us`; SONNET/HAIKU/SUBAGENT → `accounts/fireworks/routers/glm-5p3-flash-us`; base URL `https://us.api.fireworks.ai/inference`. [D560/F: Fireworks routes and credentials, 2026-09-05]
- Large-download lanes check host C: using `powershell.exe` before and after EACH file; stop below file size + 8 GB free. Linux `df` is insufficient. [F: WSL host disk guard, 2026-09-06]

- (owner) Astral Tower content scope is approximately 12 referenced SRD 5.2.1 typed monster statblocks/tests for all 26 rooms; Astraldendon/Astralmycon equivalents derive only from mechanical role, never Knave prose. Verify unfinished scope before redispatch. [D395.3]

### Roles and routing

- Codex authors substantive plans and implements; use Sol high for complex work, Terra only as fallback. A supervisor brief is not a substitute for a Codex-authored plan. [D584.5/D623/RULES.md: AGENTS.md model routing]
- (owner) `gpt-6-astra` uses `model_reasoning_effort=high` for planning/plan reviews, medium for implementation/fix reviews and judging/probes; Sol stays high. [D627]
- (owner) Supervisor owns contracts, arbitration, independent verification, gates, merges and log entries; lane self-review has no gate weight. Use a different model as decisive independent reviewer. [D135/D207/D237/D585/D623]
- (owner) Codex implements classic/resumed iso art; Fable implementation requires a stated need for its design judgment and prior owner approval. Non-art UI routing and Opus seat effort are specified in Open conflicts; the supervisor seat is Fable 5.1 (D719). [D249/D530/D532/D555/D615/D719]
- Reserve Sonnet for the verified-KB second-reader role, with no decision authority. [RULES.md: standing]
- (owner) Stop loudly on credit/usage refusal; never replace unavailable Codex with Claude. Required reviewer unavailability is HARD_GATE (c), barring implementer and reviewer work; scoring-seat failure stops without substitution or DONE. Quietstone’s scoped exception is in the experiment rules. [D586.169/D586.42/D586.43/D586.45/D587.2/RULES.md: standing]
- (owner) Spend freely on authorized model work and report totals; do not reduce cadence/depth for hypothetical token limits. [D319/D381/D394.3/D586.6/D587.2]
- (owner) Advance authorized work to the owner-action boundary; do not idle merely awaiting owner attention. Apply the owner/supervisor scope division in Open conflicts. [D137/D169/D175/D213/D323/D348/D352.3/D555]

### Consensus protocol and dispatch mechanics

- (owner) Plans receive independent critique before implementation, and each substantive increment receives independent critique; verify assumptions locally, self-critique, adjudicate findings with evidence, fix legitimate issues and resubmit with rejection reasons. First-subsystem reviews continue to clean; ordinary design disagreements cap at three rounds. [D135/D247/D604/D586.27/RULES.md: consensus]
- (owner) Continue bounded-defect rounds while significant defects remain, recording each; stop and send residuals to owner when findings-per-round stops falling. Scope reopening/design-cap overruns require owner disposition; unit-specific caps override this rule. [D604/D607.1]
- (owner) Stuck work gets independent differing-lens analyses before choosing continuation, redispatch or owner wait; no automatic strike count substitutes for diagnosis. [D136]
- Read worktree rules/state/applicable decisions and prepend maintained COMMON RULES on every public/private dispatch; prompts go through stdin files. Lanes may not invoke Claude, nested Codex or other agent CLIs, nor apply `~/.codex` consensus skills. [D207/D552/F: no-Claude preamble, 2026-09-03/F: Codex resume integrity, 2026-09-06/RULES.md: COMMON RULES]
- (owner) Use separate worktrees/databases at independent seams and one per experiment/strategy, from current main or its applicable parent lane; maximize independent fanout. Copy ignored plans into the new worktree and verify `ls <wt>/.tmp-plans` before dispatch. [D105/D248/D375/D487/D511/D586.26/F: worktree binding plans, 2026-09-04]
- Install dependencies before fresh-worktree checks; give file-producing analysis a writable workspace-local or `/tmp` path, or require stdout delivery; external symlink targets are not assumed writable. [D624.5/F: analysis output permissions, 2026-09-02/RULES.md: standing]
- Lanes perform no git/repository operations and stop for frozen/forbidden files, another lane's files, unapproved config or missing authority. Mint-free lanes change no schema/migration/wire; mint lanes use verified next registry number and synchronize schema/inventory/checksums without editing historical migrations. [D203/RULES.md: COMMON RULES]
- Generated `docs/specs/*.schema.json` changes use the real generator only, with command/diff reported; runtime Zod and generated content schema remain byte-for-value equal, with independent literal acceptance/rejection controls. Other docs require explicit scope. [D596/D635.24/F: generated schema exception, 2026-09-04]
- Plans use `YYYY-MM-DD-meaningful-name.md`, at most 80 characters; keep untracked reboot-safe copies in `.tmp/`/`.tmp-plans/`. Verify locally testable assumptions before finalizing. [D300/RULES.md: AGENTS.md]
- Resume only after a writer ends, or stop it first; use full UUID, verify printed session identity and restate full COMMON RULES. Check Fable startup logs within one minute for usage-limit failures. [F: running lane amendments, 2026-09-05/F: Codex resume integrity, 2026-09-06/F: dispatch startup verification, 2026-09-04]
- Before launching a chain inspect `ps -eo pid,lstart,args` for chain scripts themselves and record queued chains in tick state; compaction must not hide scheduled work. [F: queued chain visibility, 2026-09-03]
- Kill only numeric PIDs obtained in a prior read; a kill call contains only kills and launch is separate. Use bracketed `pgrep` patterns to prevent self-matching; never kill by a pattern present in the same command line. [D586.154/F: process termination safety, 2026-09-02/F: kill call discipline, 2026-09-07]
- Harvest only on exit-file completion and hash the finished artifact, never infer completion from a prose marker. Reviews write a `review exit:` sentinel; preserve direct runner exit statuses outside pipelines. [D586.61/D586.153/D636.2]
- A queue uses either exit-file ordering or process-presence checks, not both; sleeping queued waiters are not machine load. Queue controllers never write the gate log they await. [D586.83/D611.2]
- Stop loudly on outage, missing required credentials/input or infeasible briefs; never manufacture results. CLI-exit refusals are outage evidence even without many timeouts. [F: outage detection, 2026-09-03/RULES.md: standing]

- Supervisor tick decisions control the current queue; verify dependencies and live work before launching a historically authorized task. [D422]

### Verification, gates and evidence

- (owner) Supervisor independently runs and reads verification; label lane results CLAIMED until verified. Every compile gate/mutation check uses `npx tsc -b --force`, including test files; incremental success or an empty solution compilation is not evidence. Declaration-emit guard requires diagnostics checked and > 100 emitted files. [D237/D263.2/F19/F: forced compiler gates, 2026-09-03/F: forced compilation gate, 2026-09-03]
- Long gates write explicit exit-code done files; absent exit file or empty log means not run/killed, never passed. Save complete stdout/stderr and test summaries; no tail-only background suite evidence. [D586.141/F: long gate completion, 2026-09-04/F: never-ran gate evidence, 2026-09-06/F22]
- Cumulative inventory combines approved plan obligations, changed/new branch specs and reverse-import/affected-consumer graph; enumerate expected/discovered/executed results. Missing, skipped or undiscovered required evidence blocks acceptance; use each file's actual runner, including compile-only and browser tests. [D584/D584.3/D584.4/D586.46/D633]
- Test inputs must be declared through `tests/helpers/test-inputs.ts`, `test-filesystem.ts` or `test-filesystem-promises.ts`, never raw `node:fs`/`node:fs/promises`; source reads use the source-file capability, never unrelated declaration categories; reject ignored/untracked prerequisites and exercise isolated fixture loading. [D584.22/D586.70/D576@2026-09-07T18:54]
- Supervisor harvests run their own `sg scan`, even spikes; contracts include it. Run `sg scan src tools tests`, sg fixtures, architecture self-test/scan and M-3 suites in batch/cumulative verification, failing first. [D583.14/D584.23/D617.20]
- `test:gate` is `node scripts/check-command-outcomes.mjs && node tools/gate-vitest.mjs`; shell wrapper delegates. Keep stock JSON reporters with gate-owned `.evidence.json` sidecars and four-domain verdicts. [D617.20/D622.1]
- (owner) Supervisor owns full integrated gates; lanes run cumulative contracts and appropriate single browser specs through wrapper config. Main/UI/snapshot landings get full Playwright; non-UI lanes get touched browser specs. Every main merge gets full integrated gate. [D302/D375/D510.2/D526/D584.2/D586.34/D576.3@2026-09-07T19:10]
- Serialize full Vitest, Playwright and production builds through `/tmp/dnd-gate.lock`; runners acquire it themselves, never outer `flock`. Keep unique worktree-derived browser ports; no Vitest while Playwright owns the lock. [D105/D511/D584.2/F: gate lock deadlock, 2026-09-07]
- (owner) Run gates while independent lanes continue; each timeout-red gets exactly ONE serial rerun (`--no-file-parallelism`) before counting. Report both outcomes, never assume retry cause, and never re-pin budgets for load. Quiet same-box runs remain mandatory for performance evidence. [D264/D587.3/D586.77/D632/D644]
- (owner) Timeout increases are allowed only for named exceptions. D544: conversation “three rounds”; experiment-orchestrator “failed_result_recorded_as_pass” (5 s) / its top-level `beforeAll`; arena three-room smoke (120 s); brutal byte-cap timing; iso atlas (1.5 s); acceptance click (50 s).
  D606/D613: arena “suppresses every intel context surface while attributing off rows and preserving full bytes” → 60 s; D613’s two marginal arena cases 30 → 60 s; rl-generate-data “generates an unfrozen seed…” 30 → 60 s; engine-mcp-server “bounds one-to-three blind attempts…” 5 → 15 s.
  D635.32: only “is versioned, uses generic classic-board conventions, and leaks no non-rendered probe identity” at `tests/unit/tools/ai-dm-screenshot-probe.test.ts:464`, 5,000 → 10,000 ms; cost reduction stays queued. All other budgets remain fixed. [D544/D584.25/D606/D613/D635.32]
- Process/global timedout, interrupted, onError and onProcessTimeout are fatal; individual Playwright timeouts alone retain retry eligibility. Report `passedOnRetry`, `failedFiles`, `phaseFailures`; do not turn process failure into a test flake. [D622.1]
- Revision drift (`revisionBefore != revisionAfter`) is failed before stale filtering; stale/not-run only applies to completed equal-revision evidence differing from current HEAD. Validate sidecar version/kind/phase/invocation before extracting identities or authorizing READY. [D623.4/D623.6/D623.8]
- Before relaunch reap gate servers and check derived port with `ss -ltnp`/`fuser -n tcp`, including 4870; killing the gate group does not guarantee webServer cleanup. Preserve owner port 4173. [D586.75/F: gate server cleanup, 2026-09-07]
- (owner) No assertions weakened, waits added, skip/todo/ignore/any escapes introduced or config changed merely for green. Generation during module collection before timeouts apply is budget evasion; fix performance within budget or stop with measurements. [D7/D9/D25/D586.60/F: browser regression investigation, 2026-09-08/D576@2026-09-08T04:55]
- (owner) Delete tests only when their subject is removed; enumerate incidental coverage and re-home retained responsibilities. Independent oracles must still fail; never regenerate expectations from production output. [D7/D9/D25/D29/F10]
- Every load-bearing assertion needs a named compiling runtime mutation and exact killer; type-rejected mutants prove no runtime behavior. Scripts fail loudly on missing targets, print changed lines/counts, and verify the mutant reaches the tested surface. [F25/F: runtime mutation evidence, 2026-09-03/F: mutation script guards, 2026-09-03/RULES.md: COMMON RULES]
- Mutation scripts restore exact bytes in the same script, write RESTORED, then rerun killers; untracked files require explicit backups. Never mutate during a gate on that tree; never `git checkout -- <path>` over uncommitted lane work. [F28/F: new-file mutation restoration, 2026-09-02/F: mutant restoration, 2026-09-06]
- (owner) Surviving mutants go in commit and decision records with required next-increment killing test; ordinary survivor alone does not block landing. The explicit release mutation bar remains separately binding. [D539/D391.2]
- (owner) Fresh full mutation audits include static mutants; iterative shard reruns use ignoreStatic and the per-shard incremental cache, reporting skipped counts UNMEASURED. Classify survivors as killed/compile-error/equivalent with proof/explicitly excluded; ordinary constructible inputs define reachability. [D280/D282/D308/D310/D334.3]
- (owner) Delete unreachable paths, including hypothetical future-data arms; reachable untested paths need tests. Keep snapshot-once/captured-intrinsic public boundary hardening; structured-clone interiors trust their boundary, without hostile Proxy/intrinsic arms races. [D263.3/D281/D282]
- For matcher/validator changes add lawful-payload acceptance controls. State the behavioral claim and falsifier, inspect actual mechanism, and read fixture behavior before asserting proof; counts, matching scores and branch differences do not establish causal equivalence/authorship. [D582/D622.3/D648/F16/RULES.md: standing]
- Reproduce reviewer diagnoses and mutant attributions before accepting them; distinguish aggregate sight from individual rays. A test red in both A/B arms proves nothing about the changed variable. [D617.34/D635.3/D648]
- Read untruncated failure lists AND Tests summaries before declaring the complete red set; attribute every consequence before landing. Change running gate tooling only after its script finishes; `head -400` is not a completeness guarantee. [D635.47/D635.48/D635.50]
- Preserve independently captured legacy-output oracles: supervisor captures on clean approved main with commit/tree/tool/clean provenance, proves reversible encoding and two-checkout equality, then records fixture hash independently. Comparing two current outputs or a pin to its production constant is insufficient. [D584/D584.16/D586.32]
- Deliberate pin changes, including chrome-overlay pins, require prior authorization, old-byte demonstration and independent same-commit invariants; normalize only proved differences, preserve old values/comments and unrelated-field negative controls. Lost regression protection is never repaired by deleting a pin. [D569/D616/D626.7/D626.9/D635/D635.42/D643]
- Re-author control-flow fixtures minimally from a failing baseline, preserving intended preconditions/assertions and cause-naming comments; hand-list before/after visibility and unchanged pairs. Avoid whole-board relocation or unsupported 1000-temp-HP props. [D635.18/D635.20]
- Generated API-facing schemas need invariants and a live single-call pre-arm probe; strict schemas list every property in required, with enum kinds from one source. Every emitted context validates against its outputSchema. [F: generated API schemas, 2026-09-04/F: adjustment schema defect, 2026-09-07]
- Arena row types derive from runner types with key-set proof; a field is shipped only after persisted arena rows pass the packet builder in tests. MCP-spawning evidence requires writable supervisor checkout and actual child execution, not a fast-failing child. [D586.106/F: persisted experiment rows, 2026-09-04]
- Provide Spec/Affected/Why tables for changed Playwright specs. Before host-path production merges, inspect diffs and run live smoke; afterwards run 10-room × 1 shape-stat mini-A/B versus era reference, distinct from judged quality. [D429.1/F: pre-merge smoke omission, 2026-09-01/RULES.md: COMMON RULES]
- Mini-A/Bs run alone, without other gates/mini-A/Bs, at comparable 120 s wall; load-confounded deviations are weather until quiet replication. API-bound regular experiments may overlap only under the patched mandatory-MCP/infrastructure-failure contract. [D591/F: mini-A/B isolation, 2026-09-06]
- (owner) Private regression script lives privately, pins public SHA and blocks merge even for legitimate SRD changes; explicit justified re-pin only. Public scripts know no private path; coupling goes private → public. [D239/D244]
- (owner) Every MAJOR wrong number, silent unknown, dead end or data-integrity lie blocks v1; v1 waits for merged shared-engine/bar tracks and full audit/mutation, zero unexplained survivors in seven bar-critical VTT modules; legacy-sim survivors do not block. [D270/D279/D294/D391.1/D391.2]

- (owner) Preseeded database images may optimize tests only through an opt-in per-worker cloning helper with proven image equivalence; seed/migration/digest/corruption tests retain fresh DDL. [D306]
- (owner) Keep `scripts/patch-stryker-vitest-runner.mjs`, pinned 9.6.1, applied after npm install for import-crash misreporting; do not post the stryker-js #6150 confirmation comment unless owner reopens it. [D275/D387]
- File budgets bound review units; they never authorize leaving a confirmed production regression unfixed. Obtain an explicit scope amendment when the fix crosses the budget. [D586.188]

- (owner) A CLEAN review certifies its actual production tree; changed production needs current-tree review, and the simcore release bar requires two consecutive quiet zero-High/P1 rounds. Tests-only commits do not reopen that production-review count. [D252/D271]
- Blind-renderer verification includes `engine-mcp-boundary.test.ts` using engine-safe sources; no reducer/RNG runtime import may leak into standalone MCP. [D573@2026-09-07T09:40]
- Before a geometry change, vane-warren needs a complete asserted corner-fan vector instead of a prose-only derivation. Preserve visual requirements and repair slower geometry within unchanged budgets. [D576.3@2026-09-07T22:25]

### Git, worktrees and landing

- (owner) Merge only from MAIN worktree after checking candidate signature/status; run merge as its own command and inspect direct result. “Already up to date” unexpectedly is failure; remove worktree only after verified main landing. Preserve explicitly protected owner edits when checking cleanliness. [F23/D595/F: merge before gating, 2026-09-05]
- Supervisor runs every NEW/CHANGED spec once on a detached candidate-commit worktree, HEAD equal to candidate, no branch ref and no `.tmp-plans`; lane-local files cannot establish landing safety. [D586.131]
- (owner) Prune merged idle worktrees only after auditing unlanded work; touched dead members may be deleted and reported for supervisor verification. Never reset/clean/stash or change branches under a running agent. [D207/D251/D589]
- (owner) Push main in full to private `mirror` (`github.com/derrickschoen/srd55-private`) after every landing and whenever it lags, via `git push mirror main`; no content filtering or new approval. Lane-branch pushes require asking; private → public remains expressly blocked. [D251.4/D629/D640/D161]
- (owner) Public repo status from D266 does not authorize further publication/deployment. No Cloudflare deploy, public push, send, outward resource or tunnel without applicable explicit authorization; manual direct-upload only, no automatic Git deployment. Publication follows first real session and owner decision. [D121/D128/D228/D266/D286/D386.14/RULES.md: standing]
- (owner) Preserve owner art-request edits; all `art/` is now ignored/untracked scratch kept on disk, while `public/assets/art/` remains canonical tracked served CC0 final art. Do not move/rename that folder or auto-promote incoming deliveries. [D595/D628/D628.1]
- (owner) Historic EK/AT rewrite was exact scoped substitution, not general history-edit authority; pre-rewrite wt/party ancestry must be rebased, never merged, and backup deletion belongs to owner. Translate all pre-D649 SHAs through `.claude/consensus/purge-2026-09-17-commit-map.txt`. [D230/D649]
- (owner) Main and :4173 advance at gated clean checkpoints with rebuild; production review remains local and recorded with screenshots/numbers. Session-zero uses existing D228 ngrok domain only on authorized start. [D286/D392/D396.7]

- (owner) Publication-prep may create the intended organization only after the owner supplies its name; `srd55` was unavailable. This does not authorize deleting a spike repository or publishing the project. [D168]
- (owner) Static app/party authentication keeps secrets out of server code and uses pasted tokens; staging VTT relay Worker/config is separately permitted, deployment/paid plan still owner-gated. [D144/D260]

### Architecture and type-system invariants

- (owner) Replace freely: no compatibility adapters merely preserving old structure; the verification and data-safety rules still apply. Use typed absence, branded IDs, closed mechanical ranges/sets, exhaustive no-default switches, value objects and typed relations; user-authored vocabularies use known-set-plus-passthrough. [D7/D25/D38/D60/D550/F8/F10]
- Apply conventions completely within declared scope; proposed cuts are findings for arbitration. Share duplicate signatures such as Damage; mutable state uses invariant-owning objects, immutable quantities branded numbers; record/reuse worked type-fix patterns in KB. [D241/D269/D269a]
- Null is legitimate for undecided/import/builder/SRD-required states; investigate mixed concepts, optional relations and state machines before tightening. Same-cause nullable siblings become one optional relation non-null inside; completeness-reported null is not a defect. `characterLevel()` returns null only when no class rows exist. [D6/D6b/D6c/D6d/F25]
- (owner) Make malformed persisted rows unstorable with typed columns/one-source CHECK vocabulary, validate once per query boundary, throw on corrupt reads. Raw SQLite booleans are 0/1 and contracts explicitly decode them. [D13/D235/F9/F20: enum parse, 2026-08-14]
- (owner) Expected refusals return shared `Outcome<T>`/Refusal union in `src/refusals/`, exhaustive in worker/UI and wire-versioned. Only defects throw dependency-free tagged classes with literal name, readonly params, centrally derived message; each guard asserts class/params, formatting once per class. [D274/D276/D278]
- (owner) Resources have integer sheet-derived maxima and floor zero; recovery clamps at maximum, simulator logic overspend throws, expected user insufficiency returns refusal. Stateful identity stays with the pool. [D267/D269/D278]
- Use one source for SQL CHECKs; quote identifiers, require typeof bounds, and use IS when NULL defeats equality. Drizzle-kit generates CHECK-table rebuilds; for drop-plus-add, which needs a TTY, use two generated steps with the data transform between. Set foreign_keys OFF before BEGIN and check foreign keys before COMMIT; retain the RESTRICT-parent fixture. [D13/F20: SQLite rebuild, 2026-07-27/F21]
- (owner) Add indexes through real additive migrations; never wipe persisted images for index signature changes. Adopt indexes on EXPLAIN improvement unless measured endpoint regression; SQL_QUERY_LOG discovers shapes, frequency prioritizes but cannot veto. [D304/D393]
- Frozen migration checksums include transitive behavioral sources; re-pin every dependent migration after shared-guard change or narrow the claim. Canonical catalog digest covers all aggregates/transitive inputs and locates culprit after mismatch. [D226/D229]
- (owner) Boot verifies schema/quick_check/FKs/catalog on first boot or invalid stamp; stamp binds build ID+image digest and writes invalidate lazily. Never stamp ready before reconciliation or hide slow boot by timeout inflation. [D225/D229/D283]
- (owner) Second OPFS owner tab gets read-only snapshot, ownership/staleness banner and refresh request; takeover only when true owner is gone. Candidate-image audits are O(N), count Map operations, reject impossible images without arbitrary byte/row caps. [D284/H1]

## D683 — SUPERVISOR (2026-09-18 16:44): FINDING against my own work — records commit 8889b450 committed and mirror-pushed ~25 MB of full codex lane logs as "consensus reports"
Nineteen files under `.claude/consensus/` (blind-01, cohort-01, vis-field: plan-*-sol.md, impl-*-sol.md, review-*-astra*.md) had been saved earlier in the session as the ENTIRE codex stdout (session header, every tool call, the model's reading) instead of the lane's final message — the largest 5.3 MB, total ≈25 MB — and I committed them without checking sizes, then pushed. That is the same class of mistake as the `.tmp-lane-4b.log` blobs the owner had purged (D649): repository bloat that a records commit should never carry. Correction: every consensus file over 200 KB is trimmed in place to the lane's final message with a header naming the session id and the original size (commit following this entry); the blobs stay in the history of 8889b450 and are ADDED to the D674 batched purge list (`.tmp/runs/purge/` will carry the 19 paths at that commit). Rule for the supervisor: a consensus record is the final message after the last `tokens used` marker, never the log; check `git diff --cached --numstat | sort -rn | head` before every records commit.

## D694 — OWNER: monster-side omniscience applies only to perceived PCs (B) (2026-09-18 20:02)

Verbatim: "b" (to the question "how much does the monster side know about a PC it has never perceived?", options A omniscience-always / B perceived-only / C encounter-level omniscience with actor-level ignorance). Ruling: a PC the monster has never perceived contributes nothing to monster planning or advice beyond its existence on the opposing roster (name/id). D418.1's capability/resource omniscience (exact AC, HP, slots, spell availability, rescue/revive) applies once the PC has been perceived by that actor's knowledge projection (seen, or held in observation history / search memory). Positions of unseen PCs remain unknown as before. This narrows D418.1 and confirms the D691 privacy invariance (request bytes invariant under unseen-PC relocation/speed/Paralyzed/absent-token variants) as the permanent contract, not a BLIND-01 interim. Effect on work in flight: none; B4 privacy pins stand; B6/B7 inherit. The Part A intel line and the Open-conflicts (verify) entry are updated in place below. Next free id: D695.

The test-of-the-test mutant did its job (removing the `position_prose_cell` scanner entry made exactly the positive-control test fail: "expected [] to include 'prompt:position_prose_cell'" — a semantic kill of the missing check). But my reversal script asserted the insertion anchor was unique and it was not (the `position_gutter_label` literal occurs more than once in the file), so the reversal aborted and the worktree stayed MUTATED while I dispatched astra r3 — a review started on a dirty tree. Correction within the same minute: the candidate was already COMMITTED (e90c6292), so `git checkout -- <file>` was the correct and safe restore this time (D788's rule is about UNCOMMITTED lane work); the restored file's sha equals the HEAD blob; the r3 lane was killed by PID (wrapper + child), its partial log kept as `review-b3-r3-astra.aborted.log`, and r3 re-dispatched on the clean tree. Lesson added to D788's rule: a reversal script must re-insert by the exact removed text at a unique anchor OR simply restore from the commit when the candidate is committed — and the dispatch of a review must be gated on `git status --short` being empty, printed, not assumed.

(1) WORKER COUNT, wall seconds (Vitest Duration ≈ wall − 0.5s throughout): w8 107.3 / 105.4 / 105.6; w4 106.5 / 106.1 / 106.4; w3 106.5 / 106.1 / 106.9. A/A at w8: 104.9 vs 105.9. Cause, read off the Duration lines: summed test time ≈ 266–274s but wall ≈ 106s in every configuration, so the run is bound by one file of ≈106s that no worker count can split; 3, 4 and 8 workers all finish the other five files before it. Only import time moves (w8 ≈ 47s, w4 ≈ 43.5s, w3 ≈ 42.5s summed across workers — fewer workers, fewer duplicate imports) and it is hidden behind the long file. This is direct evidence FOR the D633 split batches (ranks 3, 4, 5: split the long files) and AGAINST any worker-count change to the gate; D632's exclusion stands.

(2) fsModuleCache (`--experimental.fsModuleCache`, Vitest 4.1.10; cache at node_modules/.experimental-vitest-cache, 57 MB / 476 files after populate, gitignored via node_modules): cold populate 106.7s; then on/off pairs 103.2/105.4, 103.8/105.2, 103.4/105.1 → on is faster by 1.4–2.2s (≈2%) in all three pairs, consistently outside the 1.0s A/A band. Mechanism visible in the breakdown: transform 0.80–0.85s vs 5.7s, import 41.3–41.6s vs 46.5–46.9s. Real but small on these suites because they are test-time bound; the benefit scales with import-heavy suites, and it is an EXPERIMENTAL flag with an on-disk cache that must be invalidated correctly on source change (not tested here). Informational only (D842); a gate change would be its own owner decision after the D633 batches land.

(3) MUTATION SHARD A/B (D842 item 3): NOT RECOMMENDED. The mutation runner (`scripts/mutation-shard.mjs`, 8 shards × BASE_CONCURRENCY 4 = 32 test processes on 24 CPUs) is CPU-saturated by construction, not single-file bound like the gate, so the worker-count finding does not transfer; an A/B needs two multi-hour full mutation runs on a quiet box for a result whose best case is a few percent, and the box is better spent on the B7/B8 lanes when codex reopens. Supervisor reading, to be confirmed in fix r2: the "best strategy" per state is decided by the engine rollout sidecar (32 seeds per action to terminal or horizon), not by a judge's opinion — the simulator is the ground truth for win chance; judges (D844/D845) score legality/reasoning and become auxiliary columns; the dataset keeps every tuning's action, its simulated win vector, and the judge scores, with the primary label = the action with the highest simulated win probability among Luna's tuned choices (ties and censored buckets handled per the existing rules). Cost consequence under the $500 Luna cap (D841): calls per state multiply by the tuning count; at the plan's $0.012 per-call reserve, 4 tunings × 3 samples = $0.144/state ≈ 2,980 states vs ≈ 7,150 at 5 samples — the fix round must present the state-count/tuning trade-off table and the owner picks; the rollout sidecar's compute (32 seeds × actions × tunings × states) also grows and needs its own wall-clock estimate on the box. ## D852 — OWNER (2026-09-21 16:33): DATA-01 Opus judge hard cap = $400 total

Verbatim: "Set the opus cap at $400" (after the supervisor explained the question and revised its own recommendation to $1,000). Ruling: the Claude Opus judge (D844/D845) has its OWN hard cap of **$400 total**, separate from the $500 Luna cap (D841), inclusive of judge pilot calls, retries and re-judging; the plan's stop-before-cap rule (item 11: reserve the worst case per call before dispatch, never overrun, stop loudly) applies to the judge exactly as to Luna. Sol judging stays on the codex plan (usage-limit cost, no dollar cap).

Consequences the DATA-01 fix r2 brief must carry: (1) at the recorded estimate (≈$0.10 per judge call at opus 4.8 list price, ≈10k in / ≈2k out) $400 buys ≈3,700–4,000 single-call states after an 8 % failure reserve, i.e. FEWER states than the $500 Luna cap can label (≈7,150); the plan must therefore either score all of a state's D846 tuning actions in ONE judge call (supervisor's recommendation) and size the judged corpus to the cap, or state which states go unjudged and how the training unit treats rows without an Opus column; (2) if the pilot's real receipts show a per-state cost that would leave part of the Luna-labelled corpus unjudged, the plan reports the shortfall to the owner rather than trimming judge coverage silently (D845: judges cover every labelled state); (3) judge prompt versioned, blinded, engine ranking withheld (D845 §2) — unchanged.

Supervisor correction recorded at full length: in the 14:4x report the supervisor described the DATA-01 judge panel as "fable, astra, sol" (that is the plan-review panel of D587.1, not the D844 dataset judges = opus high + sol high) and claimed $750 gave "about 40 % headroom" over the full state set; at the D845 estimate $750 vs ≈$715 is ≈5 %. Both were supervisor errors, corrected before the owner ruled.

Next owner question (one at a time): DATA-01 plan Q3 — collect primary/fallback labels only, or also the adjustment (retain/replace/replacement) labels? Next free id: D853.

## D852.1 — OWNER (2026-09-21 16:34): the $400 Opus judge cap is a STARTING cap, revisable

Verbatim: "this is a starting cap. can be updated later". Ruling as read: $400 (D852) is the initial ceiling, not a corpus-sizing decision. The plan does not shrink the judged corpus to fit $400; it sizes the judge design (one call per state covering all D846 tunings, supervisor recommendation stands) and, when the 200-state pilot's real receipts give a per-state cost, reports the projected total for full coverage and asks the owner to raise the cap if needed. The tool still stops loudly at whatever cap is current; raising it is an owner ruling recorded here, never a silent edit. Same status applies by analogy to the $500 Luna cap (D841) unless the owner says otherwise. Next free id: D853.

## D853 — OWNER (2026-09-21 17:14): DATA-01 collects adjustment decisions in v1 — retain/replace AND the reason

Verbatim: "Adjustments are important. We need to know if the plan needed to be altered and why". Ruling as read (overrides plan §10 Q3's recommendation of primary/fallback first): (a) `adjustment_disposition` (retain|replace) and `adjustment_replacement` are PAID, collected labels in the first dataset, not a stub gated for later; (b) each adjustment label must also capture WHY — the teacher's stated reason for altering (or keeping) the plan, recorded as a typed field alongside the disposition, and the typed materiality inputs the engine already computes (what changed between commit and execution) are stored as features so "needed to be altered" is answerable from the row, not only from the teacher's prose; (c) adjustment states are captured wherever the engine reaches an adjustment boundary (archive, capture-only, teacher-continuation, branch), not synthesized; (d) corrections (malformed-submission repair) stay out of v1 (plan §2, unchanged); (e) cost: adjustment calls draw on the same $500 Luna cap (D841) and $400 Opus starting cap (D852/D852.1); the fix r2 brief must give the projected split between primary/fallback and adjustment calls and the resulting state count, and report to the owner if full adjustment coverage would push either cap. Supervisor note for fix r2: the "reason" field needs its own blinded judge criterion (is the stated reason consistent with the materiality inputs?) so the judges (D844/D845) score it too. Next owner question: the state-count vs tuning-count trade-off (D846). Next free id: D854.

## D854 — OWNER (2026-09-21 17:44): DATA-01 repetition is set by statistical significance, blinded — states follow from the cap after that

Verbatim: "Make sure it is enough blind repetition that it is statistically significant" (answer to the state-count vs tuning-count trade-off). Rulings as read: (a) the number of Luna samples per state per tuning is NOT a fixed k chosen by fiat (the plan's k=5); it is set by a preregistered significance criterion the plan must state — e.g. the chosen top option must be separable from the runner-up at a stated confidence before a hard label is issued, and a state whose samples do not reach it stays none-dominant or receives more samples up to a stated ceiling (sequential sampling with a stop rule is acceptable if the rule is fixed before collection); (b) the same applies to tuning comparisons: a claim that tuning X beats tuning Y on a state must rest on enough engine rollout seeds that the difference in simulated win probability is significant at the stated level, so the 32-seed rollout count is to be justified or replaced by a seed count derived from the criterion; (c) every repetition is BLIND: option order shuffled per sample, actor/option identities hidden, no sample sees another sample's answer, the engine's recommendation withheld (D845); (d) the trade-off is resolved in this order: fix repetition by the significance criterion first, then the number of tunings (D846's small fixed set), then the state count is whatever the $500 Luna cap (D841) and $400 Opus starting cap (D852.1) buy — fewer, well-replicated states over more, under-replicated ones; (e) the pilot (200 states) reports the achieved separation and the samples it took, so the criterion's cost is known from receipts before scale; if the projected state count falls below what the training unit needs, report to the owner (the caps are revisable, D852.1) rather than loosening the criterion. Fix r2 brief must carry (a)–(e) with the exact test, confidence level and stop rule named by the plan and attacked by astra. Next owner question: the brainstorm's three housekeeping questions (gate-config umbrella under D842; generalizing the D583 digest exception; baseline-of-record). Next free id: D855.

## D883 — 2026-09-23 17:17 — PERF-02 void rule v2 (owner ruling): only outside test runs and busy databases delay or void a gate arm; m12 prewarm result

Owner, on the arms waiting behind another session's API-bound `claude -p` at 1.8 % CPU: "make a better rule." Then: "1.8% cpu on one core is nothing. Don;t pause for anything except code test runs and db cpu usage that is high enough to slow down our testing".

Rule, replacing the plan §7 void rule and the D882 procmatch fix for every remaining PERF-02 arm:
- Two classes of outside load count. (1) An active test run: a test-runner process plus every descendant. Runners are vitest, jest, mocha, phpunit, pest, paratest, pytest, stryker, `python -m unittest`, `node --test`, and `playwright|go|cargo|artisan test`. They are judged on the first three argv words, so a wrapper shell or agent prompt that mentions a runner does not count. (2) Database servers: mysqld, mariadbd, postgres, redis-server, mongod. Agent sessions, codex/claude lanes, builds and everything else never delay or void a run; their CPU is logged as `other`.
- Start: one 30 s window in which outside test runs average under 1.0 core and databases under 1.0 core.
- Void: either class at or above 1.0 core over any rolling 60 s window of the run.
- Kept by me, for the owner to strike: void when memory pressure stalls every task for over 1 s. That is a direct measurement that the run was slowed, not a process class.
- Mechanics: the gate runs in its own transient cgroup (`systemd-run --user --scope`), so its own runner processes are excluded exactly and its CPU is exact. The process table is sampled every 1 s (about 6 ms CPU per sample). An exited child's last interval is recovered from its parent's reaped-time delta. Logs go out every 5 s; the END line carries the verdict, per-class CPU and top consumers.
- Threshold: I first set test runs at 0.25 core and raised it to 1.0 during testing. The ai-security-scanner session runs `python3 -m unittest discover` at about 0.3 core, which would have voided our runs. By the owner's own standard that is noise. For scale, our gate draws 5–14 cores of a 24-core box (m12-r1 sampler).

Verified by me (synthetic, scratchpad v2test, a 20 s fake gate burning 2 cores with a child exec'd as `vitest`): clean → VALID, gate_cpu 40 s exact, own `vitest` child excluded, exit code 3 propagated. Burners started 2 s after the gate cgroup appeared:
- two vitest-named cores → VOID (1.09);
- one vitest-named core → VALID (0.54);
- two mysqld-comm cores → VOID (1.09);
- ten 2.5 s vitest runs in two parallel chains → VOID (1.18; 23.9 of 25 CPU s recovered);
- three plain cores → VALID with other_cpu 60 s, and the start was not delayed.
The start wait blocked on a 1-core vitest burner and on 2 database cores. Live check: against the running m12 gate the start wait read test=1.36 cores, start=no.

Finding against my own tooling (full length): the first v2 draft sampled every 5 s and lost the last interval of any process that exited under an outside parent. Two 15 s database burners (30 CPU s) measured 24.4 s and came out VALID when they should have voided. Fixed by 1 s sampling plus the parent reaped-time recovery above. A second draft of the scenarios started burners on a timer; when the start wait ran long, the burner ran during the wait instead of the gate. Replaced with burners triggered by the gate's cgroup appearing.

Scripts (.tmp/runs/perf-02/exp, sha256 prefixes): contention.py 7f9bad84, quietwait.py c37dcfb6, sampler2.py 793ce818, run-gate-arm.sh b44df21d, gate-summ.py c42e53f6, inventory.py 6b081294. v1 moved to exp/v1/.

C1's two runs (D882) ran under v1 with zero hits. v1 flagged any outside vitest/codex/claude -p process, so they also meet v2's test-run class; databases were not measured then.

m12 prewarm (main+X1+X2, e632a35a, v1 harness, prewarm only): initial 581.9 s, retry 37.5 s, total 619.4 s; last file ai-dm-conversation.test.ts at 582 s. Inventory, checked by me against main gate 1386678: 11,483 → 11,488, exactly X1 1 + X2 4 added, none missing. C1 r1 inventory: 54 conversation tests moved into the three split files (20 + 29 + 5), 11 net additions, none missing.

Open: X2's `node:fs` dynamic import in memo-freeze-audit.ts turns engine-boundary and session-command-transaction red in every X2 arm. That costs retry time equally in all three exploratory arms. It is fixed before any acceptance pair. m12-r1, c1mx3 prewarm and c1mx3-r1 now run under v2.
